4:T5bf,Mounted Notes interactions are rejected by the view capability broker because the production NOTES_SURFACE does not declare agent-surface. The canonical NOTES_VIEW uses that surface. The real Notes renderer loads its authenticated state successfully, but get-notes fails with ViewCapabilityDeniedError before any mutation can execute. Calendar already declares the required capability.

Reproduced in desktop Chromium and Pixel 7 mobile against candidate 6c1c25705fdb79e823717f9cb9ba835bd74b2485 while validating #30688. The app loads the emitted production Notes bundle and actual authenticated Notes API backed by isolated PGlite; surrounding shell identity/provider responses are deterministic fixtures. Both runs received a real empty Notes snapshot at revision 0 and then rejected the mounted read. No live-account or provider acceptance is claimed.

The existing DynamicViewLoader.notes-operation integration test passes a hardcoded agent-surface grant instead of the canonical Notes surface, masking the mismatch.

Fix: declare the intended grant on NOTES_SURFACE and use that production declaration in the existing mounted Notes test. Keep the capability broker unchanged and retain denial of guarded operations for legacy bundles lacking operation-context support. Acceptance includes the actual mounted read, create, exact replay, changed-payload rejection, stale-revision rejection, durable receipt identity, and unchanged rendered state after rejected edits.

Reproduced with the actual built renderer at `db15e8b5f2b93d266e803ffd4bc20115f7314641` and controlled HTTP catalog responses for both v1 plans. Desktop is readable. The mobile browser regression fails `Open billing` viewport reachability after scrolling, with the lower content clipped in the captured frame. No checkout or provider operation was performed.

Expected: mobile visitors can scroll through both plans, read the renewal and purchased-credit disclosure, and reach the billing link. Preserve the existing desktop layout and pricing behavior.

Scope: give the existing pricing page its own scroll region and add a real browser reachability regression. Before/after screenshots, the original failing trace, and final source-bound validation will be linked from the combined billing repair PR.

### Browser selection reproduction and environment

Pinned Node24.15.0/Bun1.3.14. Original canonical: `CI=true TEST_LANE=pr ELIZA_LIVE_TEST=0 SCENARIO_USE_DETERMINISTIC_MODEL=1 bun run --cwd packages/app test:e2e --list --reporter=json` without PLAYWRIGHT_WEBKIT. Original Test WebKit: same plus `PLAYWRIGHT_WEBKIT=1 --project=webkit`. Original Scenario auto: original cfd script and workflow produce `--list-auto`69 selectors; pass those filenames to the same app command with `--project=chromium --list --reporter=json`. New selection enables PLAYWRIGHT_WEBKIT=1 and partitions the unchanged full suite with ELIZA_UI_SMOKE_SHARD=1/6 through6/6.

Playwright config, runner, package manifest, and spec sources have no diff against cfd. Only deny-list prose changes; list membership is unchanged. Old Scenario keyless environment is explicitly retained in CI smoke, including empty provider keys. The old canonical runtime-selected environment was already keyless by default; explicit flags make the merged owner deterministic. Dedicated accounts/mock/real-local paths retain their distinct environments; no real-local proof is inferred from the stub/default inventory.

Result: old canonical926 + old WebKit36 = new962. Old auto252 is a subset; lost0, added0. Four selectors with no Chromium tests already ignored: applications-dropdown-contrast, audit-project-worker-contract, cloud-surfaces-aesthetic-audit, viewport-zoom-visualviewport (the last remains in mobile Chromium). Selection evidence does not certify execution, live-provider availability, or absence of runtime skips.

Evidence JSON SHA256: `8c728e48d92e897f859a95845c05dd91af91cda39f02457fbc96c662240af8ad`. Full tuple inventories retained locally in `/tmp/workflow-old-new-browser-proof.json`; summary independently recomputed during review.

### Nested personal-assistant duplicate

Both hosted client and plugin shard3 ran the same2,750-test command (2,744executed,6skips,0failures); client duplicate took17.88minutes. Pinned Vitest `list --config vitest.config.ts --filesOnly --json=<file>` with the respective PR lane flags selects exactly309files in either case. Plugin adds VITEST_UNIT_ONLY=1; neither plugin source/config nor its base Vitest config consumes it. a:T1383,Fixes #31437.

App inference settlement could reject a valid fractional markup refund: a $0.02 base reservation at 25% commits $0.0050 creator earnings, but settling $0.003 actual base cost requires a $0.0043 reversal to leave $0.0007. Rounding the raw difference independently gave incompatible ledger and projection amounts.

Version 2 receipts now record the original earning and initial/final collected creator amounts. Ledger movements use the difference between those endpoints while retaining the original six-decimal economic facts. Uncollected overage does not create creator entitlement; amounts below the ledger unit have explicit absent-ledger authority. The forward migration validates reservation/app/user/creator identity and protects the referenced original earning. Existing version 1 receipts and replay rules remain unchanged. Reservation-derived ledger keys use the locked canonical UUID, and app/user/organization UUID aliases are normalized without changing opaque idempotency keys.

The migration replaces the complete validator in one transaction to preserve its existing checks during the version transition. It is intentionally longer than typical additive migrations; splitting deployment would expose mixed validation contracts. Migration 0395 must run before deploying the new writer. The separate unpublished #31128 ledger migrations must be rebased and renumbered after this change lands.

The normal app-credit hold suite now applies the actual 0268 and 0395 migrations before exercising service calls, so receipt triggers and generated transaction keys are validated together. Reviewed validation repairs preserve real service exports, provision the schemas required by lifecycle reads, control expiry at the behavior being tested, and separate independent generated Docker shell scenarios. The JWT boundary fixture pins only its test clock while retaining real signatures and the production verifier.

Validation passed on head `107df0e739e49f119eb40abd062c3ad8597c8f99`, rebased onto `8e9fcf42d9851c5c3db6ce43920ab56f1d034c1e`: `bun install` and `bun run verify` both exit 0. The actual migrated service and migration tests passed 26 cases / 181 assertions. Earlier failing fixture runs remain preserved, and are not counted as passing evidence.

A fresh current-head, isolated PGlite readback independently records the original `0.0050` earning, actual `-0.0043` ledger adjustment, final `0.0007` creator balance, `0.996250` organization balance, and the v2 settlement receipt. b:T1256,## Summary

The evaluator rendered the planner's base context verbatim. The room roster (`ENTITIES`), the platform user record (`PLATFORM_USER_CONTEXT`), the widget syntax catalog (`uiWidgets`), the cross-room manifests (`recent-conversations`, `relevant-conversations`), `CHANNEL_TOPICS` and the first-run notice reached every in-loop evaluation call although `evaluatorTemplate` never references any of them.

Live evidence:
- Audit 2026-09-13: the evaluator user message was byte-identical to the planner's; `ENTITIES` + `PLATFORM_USER_CONTEXT` alone add ~7K chars per evaluator call.
- Live 2026-09-14: ~7.6K chars of `uiWidgets` / `recent-conversations` / `relevant-conversations` / `CHANNEL_TOPICS` / `firstRun` on every in-loop evaluation call.
- Gate proofs: the in-loop evaluator user message omits People in the Room, `PLATFORM_USER_CONTEXT` and the widget catalog but still carries `RECENT_MESSAGES`, `FACTS` and `CURRENT_TIME`; calendar create/move/delete evaluator FINISH still selects `effectReceiptIds`; RESTORE_CONTEXT reads refresh the evaluator context.

What changes:
- `provider-state.ts`: `EVALUATOR_STAGE_PROVIDER_EXCLUSIONS` names the providers the evaluator stage never reads.
- `pipeline.ts`: after the planner composition, `runV5MessageRuntimeStage1` composes a second `ContextObject` from the same already-run planner state through the existing `extraProviderExclusions` mechanism (the one ambient turns use), copies the planner composition's metadata onto it (`providerDiscoveryEnabled`, `historyReferenceEncoding`, `loadedContextProviders`, the completion-context selection), appends the same planner decision event and hands it to `runPlannerLoop` as `evaluatorContext`. The loaded context catalog reaches it too, so a reference the planner loaded never reads as still pending at evaluation time.
- `planner-types.ts`: `PlannerTrajectory.evaluatorBaseContext?` and `PlannerLoopParams.evaluatorContext?`.
- `planner-loop.ts`: the trajectory stores the evaluator composition (with the post-tool reply instruction appended when that seed is present); a planner-side `RESTORE_CONTEXT` read refreshes it through `restoreEvaluatorProviders` alongside `modelBaseContext`.
- `runtime/evaluator.ts`: `renderEvaluatorModelInput` renders `evaluatorBaseContext ?? modelBaseContext ?? context`, so callers that supply no evaluator composition render exactly as before; an evaluator `contextRequest` re-run refreshes both contexts; `restoreEvaluatorProviders` carries the restored planner provider bodies onto the evaluator composition while keeping its exclusions, without a second composition.

Policy note: this is not a cap or truncation. Whole named providers are removed via the existing `extraProviderExclusions` mechanism, and the removal is limited to the evaluator stage: the planner prompt is untouched, `evaluatorTemplate` never references the excluded providers, and `RESTORE_CONTEXT` reads are honored on both contexts (planner read and evaluator `contextRequest` re-run).

Derived from stable commits 51d30cd5d9f, 82c59fa5506 and the merge-only repairs in a3cbd5e9e26; carved onto `develop` (8e9fcf42d98) with only these hunks.

f:T713,Production backport of #31508 onto current `main` (`14d880c1b44`). The two affected runtime files were byte-identical on the production base and develop before this patch. This avoids promoting unrelated develop changes.

Provisioning-worker heartbeats created a new TCP Redis client on each cycle without releasing it. In production, one worker accumulated approximately 9,994 connections and exhausted Redis's 10,000-client limit, blocking lifecycle cleanup. Restarting the worker recovered the service but did not remove the leak.

Close Redis transports created by heartbeat publication, health reads, and capability checks in `finally`, including errors and timeouts. Caller-supplied clients retain caller ownership. Transport cleanup closes the socket directly instead of queuing `QUIT` behind stalled work. No funding, authorization, provisioning admission, or health-result policy changes.

Validation:
- New repeated/concurrent TCP connection regression fails against the prior implementation and passes with this change.
- 18 focused tests pass, covering connection cleanup, health behavior, socket recovery, errors, timeout and borrowed-client ownership.
- Cloud-shared package lint and typecheck pass (after building its login workspace dependency).
- Both the develop candidate and this exact production backport passed repository-wide `bun run verify` (exit 0). Hosted CI is pending. GitHub requires an eligible review before production merge; no deployment is claimed.
- Full `bun install` reached an unrelated native fused-inference CMake build failure after installing dependencies; this is not reported as a successful install.

Tracks the Redis blocker discovered during #31505 cleanup. Production recovery and live connection-count verification remain separate operational acceptance steps.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Preserves full-parser equivalence when streamed stage directions trigger whitespace normalization across cached prose.
- Restores readable dark-theme muted text, selected trajectory counts, and Discord labels; uses the existing typed orange button variant for hosting publish and captures the actual empty trajectory state.
- Registers the public pricing entry and documents its public-route rationale; updates the HMR harness for the current developer workspace collaborator.
- Aligns Cloud billing expectations and dedicated-upgrade requests with current prices and explicit quote acceptance; regenerates SDK routes for subscription checkout.
- Runs the real login child from source in its restart/recovery test, prepares pinned native sources for provenance tests, and aligns CodeQL action versions.
- Uses the current canonical model in latency certification and gives the existing real PGlite scheduling case the same startup allowance as its neighboring tests.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes and CI fixture/tooling repairs. No test gate or authorization boundary is disabled.

# Documentation changes needed?

No public API or usage documentation changes required.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

The original failing develop run above and the linked evidence below. Production changes are confined to the streaming parser, public entry routing, UI colors, generated SDK routes, and latency model selection; the other changes repair tests or CI preparation.

## Detailed testing steps

- Normal install and `bun run verify`: pass, pinned runtimes.
- Storybook: 1,689 good; zero broken, accessibility violations, or gate failures. The 73 stories with HTTP 404 diagnostics are a subset of the baseline's 78; zero page errors.
- App audit: 226 checks pass; 220 captured views have good verdicts. OCR: 208 verified, 12 manually inspected fixture/unavailable states; zero regressions.
- Streaming parser: 163 differential/performance cases pass.
- Cloud shared-to-dedicated upgrade and provisioning tests pass against the real local router with mock infrastructure boundaries; eight affected Cloud unit suites pass.
- Real login wallet provision/restart/recovery, 25 native provenance cases, 10 scheduling persistence cases, SDK tests, workflow lint, and CodeQL contracts pass.
- Public entry/bootstrap: 38 pass. Public route allowlist: six pass.
- Complete UI unit suite: 1,324 files and 14,448 tests pass; seven existing skips, exit 0.

16:T29b7,# Defect

Retention pruning could delete an idempotency-key index entry that had already been rebound to a newer operation. While that newer operation was still inside the 24-hour idempotency window, a caller retry was accepted and executed again instead of being deduplicated. Provider switches or runtime restarts could therefore run twice after a network retry, UI double-submit, or CLI retry.

The repository now removes an idempotency mapping only when the mapping still points to the operation being pruned. A filesystem-backed regression locks that ownership rule.

# Relates to

No issue exists for this reproduced defect; this pull request is the complete defect report and fix.

# Sync with develop

- [x] Rebased onto `origin/develop` commit `3654ba0cff4928f1a72aa5c2c89c06898755255d` with zero conflicts.
- [x] `bun run verify` was run post-sync. Its unrelated missing-dependency blocker is recorded below.

# Risks

Low. The production change only guards deletion of an in-memory key-to-operation mapping. A mapping that still points to the pruned operation is deleted exactly as before; a mapping rebound to a different operation is preserved.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`byIdempotencyKey` is a key-to-operation-ID map. When a key is reused after its 24-hour dedupe window, `create()` correctly rebinds it to the new operation. The old record still retains the same key. `pruneTerminal()` previously deleted the key unconditionally while evicting that old record, corrupting the newer binding.

The fix compares the indexed operation ID with the ID being pruned before deleting the mapping.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

No documentation change is needed; this restores the documented idempotency behavior.

# Testing

## Before: reproduced duplicate execution

Command:

```text
bun run <provided-reproduction>/repro-idem2.ts
```

Observed output before the change:

```text
 Info       [runtime-ops] Accepted op ed8a0191-86e1-4b41-8753-4b52e042fb9c kind=provider-switch tier=hot
 Info       [runtime-ops] Operation ed8a0191-86e1-4b41-8753-4b52e042fb9c succeeded
first  : accepted ed8a0191-86e1-4b41-8753-4b52e042fb9c applies= 1
pruned : 1
old gone: true
 Info       [runtime-ops] Accepted op d49be3f8-4af3-44b2-912f-2036cd7bbaf3 kind=provider-switch tier=hot
 Info       [runtime-ops] Operation d49be3f8-4af3-44b2-912f-2036cd7bbaf3 succeeded
retry  : accepted d49be3f8-4af3-44b2-912f-2036cd7bbaf3 applies= 2
```

17:T1278,The local `/dev` chat now exposes each recorded model call, provider result and tool step, while prompts execute through a separately selected normal app tab. For example, “open Notes” in `/dev` navigates the normal tab to `/notes`; the inspector stays open independently.

Related to #17030 (Chat trajectories). @lalalune — this updates the earlier viewer draft to the latest saved implementation. It remains draft while the acceptance gaps below are open.

### Changes

- Separate Details and Trajectories tabs, direct model-call and provider selectors, and contained scrolling.
- Complete recorded system/input/output text, tool schemas, arguments and receipts, with copy controls. Provider Result/Request/Raw data distinguishes empty results from text that was not recorded.
- Explicit total-run duration and model-call count on each reply; Details separates combined model-call time and included cached input. Unknown, invalid and unloaded measurements stay unknown or partial. Overlapping calls never produce a fabricated overhead estimate.
- Exact room/message correlation, lazy payload loading, cancellation and live summary polling. Recorded completion/evaluation usage is included; separate background runs stay separate. Missing usage stays unknown.
- Preserve full protected model payloads and provider text after audience/cancellation checks; retain secret redaction. Inspection itself adds no model calls.
- Relay send/stop through a same-origin, same-agent normal app tab. Automatically select one matching tab; require selection when several exist. Normal routes have no developer chrome.
- Preserve the existing settings owner gates and correct settings save/restart feedback.

### Review and validation

Current head: `90bf4f90b5ced8eaaeb050a6c042ef500056262a`.

- Current head: all 21 developer-workspace tests passed, including the 3.427s run / 2.292s combined-model example, concurrent calls, missing/invalid/unloaded usage and active duration labels.
- Current head: UI typecheck, changed-file Biome and `git diff --check` passed. The timing change adds no requests or inference calls.
- Live source integration: the exact recorded navigation rendered 50,855 input / 510 output / 3 model calls / 3.43s total, with 2.29s combined model time and 34,816 included cached input. Checked the existing inspector at its normal narrow width and at 375px; 1280px geometry was bounded, but the in-app browser's emulated wide screenshot was corrupted, so it is not counted as desktop visual acceptance.
- Previous checkpoint `b1667b18d5e8473a30bf522e2316cc0b0f13275a`: frozen installation, 98 UI/bridge/settings tests, 15 core trajectory tests, 5 protected-diagnostics tests, full repository verification (373 Turbo tasks plus subsequent audits), and commit-range gitleaks passed. Full verification and the broader visual acceptance have not been rerun on this four-file timing follow-up.

### Live evidence and limits

Supporting live conversations were inspected on the source integration checkpoint `5427f6d7d536718696f8b2fc3dd3ce17ed32ef83`; the latest timing labels were checked on source checkpoint `adcdb8fac0f`. These are source-integration checks, not live execution of this extracted PR head. They exercised greetings, Home/Notes/Calendar navigation, exact note creation/readback and correction recall using the configured real model. The local app visibly followed dev-issued navigation; provider text and copied raw calls were inspected. Current-head desktop/mobile screenshots, walkthrough video, OCR and frontend/backend logs still need to be attached before final acceptance. Raw private conversations and local configuration are excluded from this PR.

The viewer exposes an unresolved runtime cost issue: a recent “open notes” used 4 foreground calls / 78,324 input tokens / 7.218s, including an unnecessary widget-reference read and repeated handler pass. Navigation itself took 105ms. This PR does not claim that runtime cost is solved. Memory/recovery and context changes are reviewed separately in #31055; the saved CLI adapter is #31054.

### Reviewer flow

1. Run the local development app and open `/chat` and `/dev` in separate tabs.
2. Send “hi”, “open notes”, and “go home” through `/dev`; watch the normal tab.
3. Inspect each reply. Switch between calls, provider results and steps; copy complete input/output/system text. Check scroll and keyboard access on narrow and desktop viewports.
4. Check empty/unrecorded/failed/pending usage and a reply older than the summary window. Opening the viewer must not trigger inference.
5. Verify owner-only settings and that normal app routes show no developer header.

Merge/release acceptance is not requested by this draft.
18:T1326,# Relates to

Fixes #31472.

An authenticated OpenRouter or xAI `/models` response could contain invalid UTF-8 inside a model ID. The nonfatal decoder turned it into `vendor/�(`, published that ID as usable, and let the account-test consumer report an ordinary catalog success. The bounded catalog decoder now rejects invalid UTF-8. The key still reports `ok: true`; only the optional catalog reports `modelCatalogUnavailable: true`. Valid Unicode split across response chunks, empty catalogs, and complete long IDs remain supported.

# Sync with develop

- [x] Branch rebased onto `origin/develop` `88941f61c8281497f1a70c079eee4ac61719e67a`; zero conflicts.
- [x] `bun install --frozen-lockfile` completed after sync.
- [x] Root `bun run verify` passed on head `08e2e4ef954d382774f8219f5a6126e36b9411ba`.

# Risks

Low. Any invalid UTF-8 anywhere in the optional catalog now makes its metadata unavailable, even if the malformed bytes are outside an ID. This is deliberate: the catalog must be valid UTF-8 before its JSON and IDs can be trusted. Authentication and other direct providers' successful-body behavior are unchanged.

# Background

Bug fix to the `@elizaos/auth` direct-key probe; no public type or route change.

# Documentation changes needed?

No. The existing `modelCatalogUnavailable` response and UI state already express this failure.

# Testing

The new byte-level test failed before the repair for both providers, returning `modelIds: ["vendor/�("]`. After the repair:

- `packages/auth`: build, typecheck, lint:check, and format:check passed.
- `packages/auth` full suite as an unprivileged user: 21 files, 317 tests passed. Three unrelated Codex-login permission tests fail when run as root; the identical file reproduced those failures on an unchanged checkout.
- Agent account-route and UI account-hook consumer tests: 2 files, 37 tests passed.
- Root `bun run verify` passed after rebase and install.
- `git diff --check` passed.

Reviewer start: run `bunx vitest run packages/auth/src/direct-api-probe.test.ts` and inspect the controlled response-byte outcomes below. The tests call the actual exported direct-key probe, with network transport controlled at `fetch`.

# Evidence Details

The malformed-byte fixture replaces the continuation byte after `0xc3` with `0x28` inside an otherwise valid JSON model ID. Before the repair, the response remained parseable JSON and yielded a replacement-character ID. Afterward, both provider probes retain HTTP 200 authentication and omit `modelIds`. The valid response splits the two-byte UTF-8 sequence for `é` across stream chunks and returns the complete Unicode ID. No live key, provider call, or model invocation is claimed.

# Known gaps / failures

The full auth suite's three root-run permission-test failures are a runner limitation, not a change regression: the unchanged file fails identically on an unchanged checkout and all 317 package tests pass as a non-root user. No live provider operation was used; issue acceptance calls for controlled response bytes at the actual probe boundary.

19:Ta0b,Closes #31212

## What

Teach `packages/app/test/ui-smoke/onboarding-to-home.shared.ts` the writes remote adoption performs since #30989:

- `OnboardingRouteState` records every `PUT /api/config` body (`configWrites`), and `/api/first-run/status` reports `complete` when either the legacy `POST /api/first-run` fired or a config write carried `meta.firstRunComplete: true` (`hostFirstRunCompleted`).
- `installHomeRoutes` answers `PUT /api/config` with the merged config instead of falling through to the static server.
- `connectRemoteFirstRunToHome` presents the adopted host as `state: "running", canRespond: true`, which the adoption probe requires; the default smoke status omits `canRespond`.

## Why

`adoptRemoteAgentFirstRun` now probes host status, writes the completion marker through the config patch API, and re-reads first-run status to confirm it. With the old mocks the probe rejected the host, so first-run never completed, the runtime chooser stayed on screen, and `scenarios / Zero-Key app browser core` has been red on every develop run since 2026-09-11 (latest: [run 34723784763](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/34723784763/job/103635554387)). Test-harness-only change; the real-server adoption coverage from #30989 is untouched.

## Evidence

Negative control, develop's harness at `1fa9dbf22d74` (`bun run --cwd packages/app test:e2e test/ui-smoke/onboarding-to-home.spec.ts --project=chromium -g "Remote connect"`):

```
  ✘  1 [chromium] › test/ui-smoke/onboarding-to-home.spec.ts:215:3 › … › Remote connect adopts a host and replaces onboarding without the old screen
    Error: expect(locator).toBeVisible() failed
  1 failed
```

With this change, the whole spec:

```
  ✓  1 … Local onboarding lands on home and opens the Launcher pager
  ✓  2 … Cloud onboarding connects, binds an agent, and lands on the home
  ✓  3 … Local cloud-inference onboarding completes in chat
  ✓  4 … Other provider completes in chat and hands off to Settings without model download
  ✓  5 … Remote connect adopts a host and replaces onboarding without the old screen
  ✓  6 … tutorial CHOICE 'Take the tutorial' completes onboarding and launches the tour
  6 passed (44.2s)
```

Run on Node 24.15.0 with the pinned Bun 1.3.14 (the ui-smoke lane refuses older Node). `bunx biome check` on the file: clean. Root `bun run verify` is running on a stack with #31211; result will be posted here.

1c:T571,Closes #31299

## What broke

`d4fa103cbfc7` (#31292) added the reviewed workspace deletion section to `FamilyOperationsView`; its adapter reads `/api/lifeops/family-workflows/deletion` on load and the `preview` / begin / `resume` routes on interaction. The keyless smoke server has no personal-assistant services, so it answers 501, and `plugin-views-interaction.spec.ts` (`family-operations — exercise every control, no crash`) fails in `Smoke (4)` and `Zero-Key app browser view lifecycle` with `http 501: /api/lifeops/family-workflows/deletion`. Same class as #31234, one route family later.

## Change

`installDefaultAppRoutes` in `helpers.ts` now serves the deletion routes with the envelopes `deletion-contracts.ts` defines: `GET …/deletion` → `{ job: null }`, `GET …/deletion/preview` → an empty `familyDeletionPreviewSchema` document, `POST …/deletion` and `POST …/deletion/resume` → `202 { job }` with a schema-valid job. Other methods fall through as before.

## Evidence (develop `61446bb82c9b`, chromium, local ui-smoke stack)

- Baseline on develop: the family-operations case ✘ with the 501 above.
- With this change: ✓ (3.7 s), in a run that also covered #31298, #31300 and the whole `apps-builtin-pages-interactions` file (12/12).

1d:Tc86,# Relates to

Related to #31506 and the [failed develop Dev Smoke job](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/35128823244/job/104905980546).

# Sync with develop

Fetched and rebased on `deb7dea70cae27fcec65cf386e10a43a9a120007`. Normal installation uses pinned Bun 1.3.14 and Node 24.15.0; native inference setup is explicitly excluded.

# Risks

The scheduled smoke lane no longer receives the evaluation-only Cerebras credential. Its existing configured provider selector and account routing remain responsible for real inference. This does not change the Cerebras-specific latency benchmark or prove a live provider call succeeds.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Removes the Cerebras evaluation key from the Dev Smoke workflow environment. The harness deliberately skips implicit Cerebras, but the runtime endpoint resolver auto-selects it when this ambient key is present before onboarding. That mismatch sent the failing run to a quota-exhausted Cerebras endpoint even though the harness selected another provider.

The regression reads the actual workflow environment with synthetic CI credentials and exercises the real live-provider selector and OpenAI endpoint resolver. It fails before the export is removed and passes afterward. No provider implementation or model selection policy is changed.

## What kind of change is this?

CI configuration fix with a deterministic regression.

# Documentation changes needed?

The adjacent workflow comment explains why the evaluation credential must stay out of this lane.

# Testing

- 72 live-provider and account-pool tests pass, including OpenRouter compatible credential export.
- 17 GitHub action/workflow contracts pass; actionlint passes.
- Normal install and `bun run verify`: terminal pass on the stated head with pinned runtimes.
- Hosted checks and a real post-merge Dev Smoke run remain pending.

20:T44b,Closes #31302

## What broke

`apps-builtin-pages-interactions.spec.ts:119` (`learning a skill opens an editable conversation draft`, added in `61446bb82c9b`) ends with `toHaveCount(1)` on the fixture replies in the thread. The keyless stub keeps `stubConversationMessages` as a module-level `Map` for the whole run, so replies from earlier specs in the same worker are still in the thread; `Smoke (1)` sees `Expected: 1 / Received: 4`, while the spec passes alone.

## Change

The spec records the fixture-reply count before the send and expects exactly one more afterwards, the way it already treats `sentMessages`. One spec file.

## Evidence (develop `61446bb82c9b`, chromium, local ui-smoke stack, `--repeat-each=2 --workers=1` so the second pass runs against a thread the first already filled)

- Develop's assertion: pass 1 ✓, pass 2 ✘ `Expected: 1 / Received: 2` (the CI failure shape).
- With this change: both passes ✓ (see comment below for the run output).

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)

https://claude.ai/code/session_01JwaJQUMKt9LhWLhxDwVCAB
21:T4d4,Closes #31298

## What broke

`61446bb82c9b` (#31269) made `/rolodex` a legacy path of Relationships (`rolodex: { aliasOf: "relationships" }`, `legacyPaths: ["/rolodex"]` in `builtin-route-descriptors.ts`, and `view-routes.ts` already probes `/apps/relationships`). `all-pages-clicksafe.spec.ts` still expected the URL to end in `/rolodex` and waited for an unavailable-state ready check, so `Zero-Key app browser all-pages` and `Smoke (1)` fail on every develop run since (`Expected pattern: /\/rolodex$/`, `Received: …/apps/relationships`).

## Change

The `rolodex` probe now carries `expectedUrl: /\/apps\/relationships$/` and a rendered-main ready check; the retained deep link is a redirect, not an unavailable state. One spec file.

## Evidence (develop `61446bb82c9b`, chromium, local ui-smoke stack)

- Baseline on develop: `desktop rolodex` ✘, `mobile rolodex` ✘ with the URL mismatch above (log `uismoke-baseline-0914.log`).
- With this change: both ✓ (3.3 s / 2.8 s), in a run that also covered the sibling repairs #31299, #31300 and the whole `apps-builtin-pages-interactions` file (12/12).

<details>
<summary>Changelog</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/vercel-labs/json-render/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md">@​json-render/core's changelog</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>0.20.0</h2>
<!-- raw HTML omitted -->
<h3>New Features</h3>
<ul>
<li><strong>Named slots for React:</strong> Components can declare named slots such as <code>header</code> and <code>footer</code>, while <code>children</code> remains the default slot. Slots are preserved through validation, streaming, nested conversion, code export, playground views, and Devtools navigation (<a href="https://github.com/vercel-labs/json-render/tree/HEAD/packages/core/issues/320">#320</a>). Built from the original contribution by <a href="https://github.com/wotnak"><code>@​wotnak</code></a> in <a href="https://github.com/vercel-labs/json-render/tree/HEAD/packages/core/issues/105">#105</a></li>
<li><strong>Nested repeats:</strong> <code>repeat.statePath</code> now accepts item-relative paths such as <code>{ &quot;$item&quot;: &quot;employees&quot; }</code>, enabling nested data rendering across React, React Native, React Email, React PDF, Image, Ink, Solid, Svelte, and Vue (<a href="https://github.com/vercel-labs/json-render/tree/HEAD/packages/core/issues/319">#319</a>). Built from the original contribution by <a href="https://github.com/tmchow"><code>@​tmchow</code></a> in <a href="https://github.com/vercel-labs/json-render/tree/HEAD/packages/core/issues/256">#256</a></li>
<li><strong>Harness chat example:</strong> Added a complete Next.js example using the AI SDK 7 harness adapter, agent delegation, sandbox transport, and json-render components (<a href="https://github.com/vercel-labs/json-render/tree/HEAD/packages/core/issues/302">#302</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h3>Bug Fixes</h3>
<ul>
<li><strong>Chained action params:</strong> Named <code>onSuccess</code> and <code>onError</code> actions now receive their configured <code>params</code> across core and all renderer bridges (<a href="https://github.com/vercel-labs/json-render/tree/HEAD/packages/core/issues/307">#307</a>)</li>
<li><strong>Consistent optional visibility:</strong> Element <code>visible</code> fields now remain optional across Zod 4 versions, while prompts explicitly require <code>children</code> arrays for every element (<a href="https://github.com/vercel-labs/json-render/tree/HEAD/packages/core/issues/299">#299</a>)</li>
<li><strong>Spec validation and autofix:</strong> Dangling child references are pruned, malformed visibility conditions are reported, and repeated items can be filtered safely (<a href="https://github.com/vercel-labs/json-render/tree/HEAD/packages/core/issues/300">#300</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h3>Improvements</h3>
<ul>
<li><strong>Release toolchain hardening:</strong> The workspace now requires Node.js 24 and pnpm 11, enforces package engine checks, and applies a minimum package release age (<a href="https://github.com/vercel-labs/json-render/tree/HEAD/packages/core/issues/293">#293</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h3>Breaking Changes</h3>
<ul>
<li>Custom renderer bridges that implement the core <code>executeAction</code> callback must now accept an <code>ActionBinding</code> instead of a bare action name. This exposes chained action params to custom integrations at compile time (<a href="https://github.com/vercel-labs/json-render/tree/HEAD/packages/core/issues/307">#307</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h3>Contributors</h3>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://github.com/ctate"><code>@​ctate</code></a></li>
<li><a href="https://github.com/Railly"><code>@​Railly</code></a></li>
<li><a href="https://github.com/tmchow"><code>@​tmchow</code></a></li>
<li><a href="https://github.com/wotnak"><code>@​wotnak</code></a></li>
</ul>

- [x] Synced with `upstream/develop` at `886df6eba0`; zero conflicts.
- [x] The exact unrelated `bun run verify` blocker is documented in **Known gaps / failures**.

# Risks

Low. This only changes when existing test dependencies load. Production code, assertions, mocks, and timeout values are unchanged.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Loads the storage bridge, persistence layer, and Steward wallet provider during test collection instead of dynamically importing them inside 5-second test bodies. This keeps module transformation outside the operation budget the tests are meant to enforce.

These are existing integration contracts, not coverage-only checks. They protect two observable failures: native sign-out must not finish before secure credential deletion commits, and wallet configuration must stay fail-closed when no valid WalletConnect project ID exists. The full UI suite already owns broader rendering and navigation behavior; these files own the storage and connector boundaries directly, using the real modules and unchanged assertions.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix to the UI test harness.

# Documentation changes needed?

No. There is no user-facing or API behavior change.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

- `packages/ui/src/bridge/storage-bridge-secure-contract.test.ts`
- `packages/ui/src/cloud/billing/wallet/wallet-connect-project-id.test.ts`

## Detailed testing steps

```bash
bun run --cwd packages/ui test -- src/bridge/storage-bridge-secure-contract.test.ts src/cloud/billing/wallet/wallet-connect-project-id.test.ts
bun run --cwd packages/ui typecheck
```

Focused tests: 2 files passed, 26 tests passed. The final run spent 27.26s importing modules and 701ms in test bodies, so the existing 5-second budgets remain meaningful and unchanged.

Before this change, the same focused command timed out in the active-server teardown test after 5 seconds (25 passed, 1 failed).

</details>25:T1717,Bumps [coding-agent-adapters](https://github.com/HaruHunab1320/coding-agent-adapters) from 0.16.3 to 0.17.0.
<details>
<summary>Changelog</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/HaruHunab1320/coding-agent-adapters/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md">coding-agent-adapters's changelog</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>[0.17.0] - 2026-06-16</h2>
<h3>Added</h3>
<ul>
<li><strong><code>OpencodeAdapter</code></strong> — first-class adapter for <a href="https://opencode.ai">OpenCode</a>, a provider-agnostic coding agent that speaks OpenAI's chat-completions protocol against any compatible endpoint (Anthropic, OpenAI, Cerebras, OpenRouter, Groq, Together, DeepSeek, Ollama, vLLM, plus native Anthropic). <code>adapterType = 'opencode'</code>, <code>displayName = 'OpenCode'</code>, <code>memoryFilePath = 'AGENTS.md'</code>. Implements <code>detectLogin</code>, <code>detectBlockingPrompt</code>, <code>detectLoading</code>, <code>detectTaskComplete</code>, <code>detectReady</code>, <code>parseOutput</code>, <code>getPromptPattern</code>, <code>getHealthCheckCommand</code> against opencode 1.14.x TUI output patterns. Non-interactive runs use <code>opencode run --dangerously-skip-permissions &lt;task&gt;</code> with the task as a positional arg (from <code>config.adapterConfig.initialPrompt</code>); interactive runs launch the TUI. Recommended model picks claude-opus-4-7 when an Anthropic key is present, gpt-4o otherwise. Thanks <a href="https://github.com/RemilioNubilio"><code>@​RemilioNubilio</code></a> (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/HaruHunab1320/coding-agent-adapters/issues/3">#3</a>).</li>
<li><code>'opencode'</code> added to <code>AdapterType</code> union and <code>ADAPTER_TYPES</code> registry; <code>createAllAdapters()</code> now returns 6 adapters.</li>
<li><code>generateOpencodeApprovalConfig(preset)</code> — only <code>autonomous</code> adds <code>--dangerously-skip-permissions</code>; other presets stay interactive.</li>
<li><code>BASELINE_PATTERNS</code> entry for opencode in <code>pattern-loader.ts</code>.</li>
<li>24 shape tests for <code>OpencodeAdapter</code> covering properties, command/args/env, login detection, blocking prompts, state detection, health check.</li>
</ul>
<h3>Changed</h3>
<ul>
<li><strong>Claude <code>autonomous</code> preset no longer emits <code>--tools &lt;list&gt;</code>.</strong> The hardcoded <code>CLAUDE_TOOL_CATEGORIES</code> list reflected Claude Code's dev-tier tool registry (<code>Bash</code>, <code>Edit</code>, <code>Write</code>, <code>Task</code>, <code>Skill</code>, …). On the claude.ai OAuth subscription tier the registry differs (<code>Monitor</code>, <code>ScheduleWakeup</code>, <code>ToolSearch</code>, <code>EnterPlanMode</code>, <code>Cron*</code>, …), so passing the dev-tier list via <code>--tools</code> filtered subscription-tier agents down to a tiny read-only subset (<code>Read</code>, <code>Grep</code>, <code>Glob</code>, <code>AskUserQuestion</code>, <code>TodoWrite</code>) — the opposite of what &quot;autonomous&quot; means. With <code>--dangerously-skip-permissions</code> already set on the autonomous preset, the explicit allowlist was redundant; dropping it makes the preset tier-agnostic. Thanks <a href="https://github.com/RemilioNubilio"><code>@​RemilioNubilio</code></a> (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/HaruHunab1320/coding-agent-adapters/issues/1">#1</a>). <strong>Behavior change for callers that inspected <code>cliFlags</code> for <code>--tools</code></strong> — that flag is no longer emitted on the autonomous preset.</li>
</ul>
<h2>[0.16.4] - 2026-05-26</h2>
<h3>Changed</h3>
<ul>
<li>Repository extracted from the parallax monorepo into its own standalone repo at <code>github.com/HaruHunab1320/coding-agent-adapters</code>. Package metadata (<code>repository</code>, <code>homepage</code>, <code>bugs</code>) updated accordingly. No source code changes.</li>
<li>Removed the (optional, workspace-only) <code>agent-adapter-monitor</code> peer dependency. The helper was never published to npm and the import inside <code>pattern-loader.ts</code> is gated by an optional dynamic require, so callers that did not install it are unaffected.</li>
</ul>
</blockquote>
</details>

</ul>

<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/nltk/nltk/releases">nltk's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>v3.10.3</h2>
<p>Version 3.10.3 2026-08-12</p>
<ul>
<li>docs: wrap Chat-80 HOWTO output</li>
<li>Sandbox Stanford JAR execution to nltk_data directories</li>
<li>Harden path-traversal / file-I/O sandbox: close write-side symlink TOCTOU + shared-temp squat, lock the cluster with a living audit (CWE-22/59/377)</li>
<li>Extend algorithmic-complexity DoS hardening: repo-wide sweep + two-string distances (CWE-407/CWE-400)</li>
<li>Bound unbounded-work DoS in parsers and grammar transforms (CWE-407/674/835)</li>
<li>fix(security): sandbox MaltParser's Java execution (CVE-2026-12252, CVE-2026-12841)</li>
<li>fix(security): trust the system temp dir only when it is private (CWE-377/CWE-378)</li>
<li>fix(security): validate corpus-reader roots against the data sandbox (CWE-73)</li>
<li>fix(security): validate per-call java() options and replace the -XX:/-D allowlist with a minimal one (CWE-88)</li>
<li>Additional security hardening (CWE-407, CWE-426, CWE-427, CWE-502, CWE-59, CWE-776, CWE-918)</li>
</ul>
<p>Thanks to the following contributors to 3.10.3: Mohammad Favas S, leduckhuong, Ziyu Lin, dougtrainer28-cmyk, Chaitanya Kadian, 0xRenSec, Arpit Jain, Jace, nguyencanhthuong, Liling Tan, medimedi, Eric Kafe.</p>

</ul>

</details>27:T605,Closes #31215

## What

In `.github/workflows/test.yml`, run the app-core screenshot-quality diagnostics file directly:

```
bunx vitest run --config packages/app-core/vitest.config.ts packages/app-core/test/app/screenshot-quality.test.ts
```

instead of `bun run --cwd packages/app-core test -- test/app/screenshot-quality.test.ts`, matching the neighbouring app step.

## Why

The app-core `test` script is a `&&` chain, so Bun appends the file path to its last command (`bun run test:script-suites test/app/screenshot-quality.test.ts`) and vitest runs the whole suite. That suite now includes `steward-sidecar-login.test.ts`, which needs the `@elizaos/login` workspace the diagnostics job never builds, so `tests / Zero-Key diagnostics` fails on a test the step was never meant to run (latest: [run 34723784763](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/34723784763/job/103635168222): `1 failed | 365 passed`). Workflow-only change.

## Evidence

Expansion of the old invocation (first line Bun prints, locally on develop `1fa9dbf22d74`):

```
$ node ../scripts/run-vitest.mjs run --config vitest.config.ts && bun run test:script-suites test/app/screenshot-quality.test.ts
 RUN  v4.1.10 …/packages/app-core
```

New invocation from the repository root:

```
 Test Files  1 passed (1)
      Tests  2 passed (2)
   Duration  724ms
```

The workflow still parses (PyYAML load; the step's `run` reads as above).

</details>2b:Tc3c,The zero-key diagnostics job accidentally ran the full app-core suite, then failed because its Node script tests imported an unbuilt skills parser. This change selects the intended screenshot test and makes the standalone script suite build its canonical prerequisites.

# Relates to

Fixes #31530. Related to #31506. Reproduced in [develop diagnostics job](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/35154166652/job/104990486221).

# Sync with develop

Based on `cfd144bf95af02590e8707259ad6db8f48edad1d`; normal installation and root verification passed. Final fetch/rebase confirms the same base and unchanged source.

# Background

Passing a filename to app-core's composite `test` script did not forward it to the Vitest invocation. The workflow now uses the repository's direct Vitest wrapper with the intended config and test file. `test:script-suites` invokes the skills build task; Turbo supplies its declared core, logger, and cloud-routing prerequisites. It does not select the entire workspace package graph.

# Risks

The standalone script suite now requires the build toolchain already installed by the repository setup. Build errors stop the producer before its tests. No production runtime, provider, prompt, or test assertions change.

# Documentation changes needed?

No public API or product behavior changes.

# Testing

- Normal installation passed with Bun 1.3.14 and Node 24.15.0; native inference compilation was explicitly excluded.
- The real packaged-parser test failed before the prerequisite build with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`, then passed through the repaired entry point.
- Four canonical build tasks passed, followed by 99 Node tests and 24 Bun tests.
- The selected screenshot-diagnostics file passed both tests; Actionlint passed.
- The unchanged app-core unit source passed 4,630 tests in the linked develop job (14 skipped); its later script-prerequisite failure is retained as the before evidence.
- Root verification passed: 373 Turbo tasks and every final audit. Hosted exact-head validation remains required before merge.

<blockquote>
<h2>v4.37.9</h2>
<ul>
<li>Update default CodeQL bundle version to <a href="https://github.com/github/codeql-action/releases/tag/codeql-bundle-v2.26.4">2.26.4</a>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/4106">#4106</a></li>
</ul>
<h2>v4.37.8</h2>
<p>No user facing changes.</p>

<details>
<summary>Changelog</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/github/codeql-action/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md">github/codeql-action/init's changelog</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h1>CodeQL Action Changelog</h1>
<p>See the <a href="https://github.com/github/codeql-action/releases">releases page</a> for the relevant changes to the CodeQL CLI and language packs.</p>
<h2>[UNRELEASED]</h2>
<p>No user facing changes.</p>
<h2>4.37.9 - 26 Aug 2026</h2>
<ul>
<li>Update default CodeQL bundle version to <a href="https://github.com/github/codeql-action/releases/tag/codeql-bundle-v2.26.4">2.26.4</a>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/4106">#4106</a></li>
</ul>
<h2>4.37.8 - 21 Aug 2026</h2>
<p>No user facing changes.</p>
<h2>4.37.7 - 13 Aug 2026</h2>
<ul>
<li>Update default CodeQL bundle version to <a href="https://github.com/github/codeql-action/releases/tag/codeql-bundle-v2.26.3">2.26.3</a>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/4085">#4085</a></li>
</ul>
<h2>4.37.6 - 04 Aug 2026</h2>
<ul>
<li>Changed the default filepath for the new remote file address format that was introduced in CodeQL Action 4.37.0 / 3.37.0 to <code>.github/codeql-config.yml</code> to align it with the suggested path that is used elsewhere. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/4070">#4070</a></li>
</ul>
<h2>4.37.5 - 03 Aug 2026</h2>
<ul>
<li>Fixed a bug where a network error while streaming the download of the CodeQL bundle could terminate the <code>init</code> Action instead of falling back to downloading the bundle before extracting it. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/4061">#4061</a></li>
</ul>
<h2>4.37.4 - 29 Jul 2026</h2>
<ul>
<li>This version of the CodeQL Action adds support for the <code>tools</code> input for the <code>codeql-action/init</code> step to be specified using a <code>github-codeql-tools</code> <a href="https://docs.github.com/en/organizations/managing-organization-settings/managing-custom-properties-for-repositories-in-your-organization">repository property</a>. This feature will gradually be rolled out following the release of this version. Once rolled out, this allows for the CodeQL CLI version that is used in GitHub-managed workflows, such as Default Setup, to be set to a custom value. For example, customers who run into issues with rate limits when a new CodeQL CLI version is released can set the value to <code>toolcache</code> to always use the CodeQL CLI version that is available in the runner toolcache. For Advanced Setup workflows, the value provided for <code>tools</code> in the workflow definition always takes precedence unless the value of the repository property starts with <code>!</code>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/4037">#4037</a></li>
<li>Update default CodeQL bundle version to <a href="https://github.com/github/codeql-action/releases/tag/codeql-bundle-v2.26.2">2.26.2</a>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/4051">#4051</a></li>
</ul>
<h2>4.37.3 - 22 Jul 2026</h2>
<p>No user facing changes.</p>
<h2>4.37.2 - 21 Jul 2026</h2>
<ul>
<li>The new address format for the <code>config-file</code> input that was introduced in CodeQL Action 4.37.0 is now enabled by default. In addition to the format described there, the <code>remote=</code> prefix can now be used to explicitly indicate that the input refers to a remote file. All previous input formats continue to be accepted as well. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/4023">#4023</a></li>
<li>The CodeQL Action can now make use of <a href="https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/how-tos/secure-at-scale/configure-organization-security/manage-usage-and-access/giving-org-access-private-registries">configured private registries</a> in Default Setup to retrieve CodeQL configuration files from remote repositories that require authentication. This will allow customers to store their CodeQL configuration in a single repository that can then be referenced by Default Setup workflows in other repositories. We expect to roll this and other, related changes out to everyone in July. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/4007">#4007</a></li>
</ul>
<h2>4.37.1 - 16 Jul 2026</h2>
<ul>
<li><em>Upcoming breaking change</em>: Add a deprecation warning for customers using CodeQL version 2.20.6 and earlier. These versions of CodeQL were discontinued on 1 July 2026 alongside GitHub Enterprise Server 3.16, and will be unsupported by the next minor release of the CodeQL Action. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3956">#3956</a></li>
<li>Update default CodeQL bundle version to <a href="https://github.com/github/codeql-action/releases/tag/codeql-bundle-v2.26.1">2.26.1</a>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/4019">#4019</a></li>
</ul>
<h2>4.37.0 - 08 Jul 2026</h2>
<ul>
<li>Update default CodeQL bundle version to <a href="https://github.com/github/codeql-action/releases/tag/codeql-bundle-v2.26.0">2.26.0</a>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3995">#3995</a></li>
</ul>
<!-- raw HTML omitted -->
</blockquote>

# Sync with develop

Rebased on `1db607dba6e750f4dd103cee18f3617d9e1e54b0`; normal install and root verification pass after rebase with Bun 1.3.14 / Node 24.15.0.

# Background

The Lerna update resolved the root YAML parser to 4.3.0. Restore the previously accepted 4.3.1 resolution. Normal Bun installation retains it and removes redundant nested copies of that same parser and argparse; consumers requiring major versions 3 and 5 retain their own versions. Lerna remains at 10.0.1. No manifest, guard, or advisory threshold changes.

# Risks

Lerna declares an exact 4.3.0 dependency, as its previous release also did; this preserves the repository's earlier remediated resolution. A future lock refresh must continue passing the existing guard. No global override forces incompatible parser majors onto other consumers.

# Documentation changes needed?

No usage or public API changes.

# Testing

- Normal and frozen installation pass; root parser stays 4.3.1.
- All three existing dependency-lock security guards pass unchanged.
- Actual Lerna CLI reports 10.0.1.
- Root verification passes before and after rebase; native inference compilation explicitly excluded from installation.
- Hosted validation remains required before merge.

Related to #31506. Fixes the credential-pool visual failure in [develop run 35143117657](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/35143117657/job/104953188173).

# Sync with develop

Rebased on `1db607dba6e750f4dd103cee18f3617d9e1e54b0`. Bun 1.3.14 and Node 24.15.0; normal installation excludes native inference compilation.

# Background

The Contribute Key button used the default black variant, so the real credential-pool browser producer failed both orange rest and darker-orange hover assertions. Use the existing `accentDarkHover` variant. Credential storage, provider validation, invitation, and removal behavior are unchanged.

# Risks

Low: one existing button variant changes. The real local Cloud router and PGlite browser flow covers invalid-key rejection, valid-key creation, disabling, invitations, and removal.

# Documentation changes needed?

No public API or usage changes.

# Testing

- Focused credentials and button tests: 24 pass.
- Credential-pool browser producer: all assertions pass; desktop/mobile captures and walkthrough inspected. An additional capture waits for the actual CSS transition before checking mobile hover.
- Root verification: 373 tasks pass. App build passes; app audit 226 tests pass, with zero broken or needs-work views. OCR: 208 verified and 12 manually inspected expected unavailable/fixture states.
- Hosted validation must finish before merge. The full 14,448-test UI suite passed in the preceding shared-variant repair; this one-line consumer change uses focused tests and the real browser flow.

<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/github/codeql-action/releases">github/codeql-action/init's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>v4.38.0</h2>
<ul>
<li>On GitHub-hosted runners, the CodeQL Action now deletes unused CodeQL bundles from the toolcache before downloading a different bundle, which frees up disk space for the analysis. We expect to roll this change out to everyone in September. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/4124">#4124</a></li>
<li>The CodeQL Action now supports CodeQL releases that are compatible with Linux Arm64 and downloads the native <code>linux-arm64</code> CodeQL bundle when available. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/4072">#4072</a></li>
<li>Update default CodeQL bundle version to <a href="https://github.com/github/codeql-action/releases/tag/codeql-bundle-v2.27.0">2.27.0</a>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/4129">#4129</a></li>
</ul>
<h2>v4.37.9</h2>
<ul>
<li>Update default CodeQL bundle version to <a href="https://github.com/github/codeql-action/releases/tag/codeql-bundle-v2.26.4">2.26.4</a>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/4106">#4106</a></li>
</ul>
<h2>v4.37.8</h2>
<p>No user facing changes.</p>
</blockquote>

<details>

<h1>CodeQL Action Changelog</h1>

<h2>[UNRELEASED]</h2>
<p>No user facing changes.</p>
<h2>4.38.0 - 09 Sept 2026</h2>
<ul>
<li>On GitHub-hosted runners, the CodeQL Action now deletes unused CodeQL bundles from the toolcache before downloading a different bundle, which frees up disk space for the analysis. We expect to roll this change out to everyone in September. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/4124">#4124</a></li>
<li>The CodeQL Action now supports CodeQL releases that are compatible with Linux Arm64 and downloads the native <code>linux-arm64</code> CodeQL bundle when available. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/4072">#4072</a></li>

<p>No user facing changes.</p>

In addition to the format described there, the <code>remote=</code> prefix can now be used to explicitly indicate that the input refers to a remote file. This will allow customers to store their CodeQL configuration in a single repository that can then be referenced by Default Setup workflows in other repositories. We expect to roll this and other, related changes out to everyone in July. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/4007">#4007</a></li>

## What broke

`61446bb82c9b` (#31269) imported `createZipArchive` from `@elizaos/agent/api/zip-utils` in the ui-smoke helpers. Outside the `eliza-source` condition that export resolves to `packages/agent/dist/api/zip-utils.js`, and `packages/app` does not depend on `@elizaos/agent`. The dist-less unit lane (`tests / Zero-Key unit + UI coverage`, `bun run --cwd packages/app test`) loads the Playwright config, which imports the helpers, so `playwright-test-match.test.mjs` fails with `Cannot find module …/@elizaos/agent/dist/api/zip-utils.js` and `No tests found`.

## Change

One import: the zip writer comes from `../../../agent/src/api/zip-utils` (dependency-free source module), the pattern `test/dev-smoke/live-onboarding.ts` and `test/electrobun-packaged/live-api.ts` already use for app-core. Playwright transpiles it like the rest of the harness.

## Evidence

- `scripts/playwright-test-match.test.mjs` with `packages/agent/dist` moved aside: fails on develop's import (the CI error), passes with this change; passes on both with dist present (see comment below for the exact runs).
- The ui-smoke stack with this import in place ran 12/12 across `all-pages-clicksafe`, `plugin-views-interaction` and `apps-builtin-pages-interactions` on develop `61446bb82c9b`, so the export mock still produces a readable archive.

https://claude.ai/code/session_01JwaJQUMKt9LhWLhxDwVCAB
32:Tfbb,A replacement response with a missing, malformed, unresolved, or inactive `visualContinuation` field can leave the previous response's navigation decision staged. The next evaluator then consumes that discarded decision instead of using the authorized catalog/classifier fallback. Clear the previous same-message decision before parsing, and when the field is inactive. Valid replacements remain single-use; active prompt composition does not evict them.

Fixes #31400. Related: #31332, #31330. @lalalune

Develop now includes the broader same-turn navigation reuse implementation. This PR preserves its original history through an ordinary merge and keeps develop's newer behavior; the remaining diff is four files for the redispatch safeguard and regression coverage. It does not add a prompt, model call, utterance shortcut, or provider change to ordinary valid turns.

## Sync, scope and risk

Ordinary merge of develop `886df6eba0c33e40da1794f5e632da6c094bedea`, as requested by the owner; no rebase or force-push. The paired plugin guides document the lifecycle contract. Risk is navigation-decision lifetime during retries. Runtime, message, actor, room, complete-text and role bindings, cancellation, catalog checks and domain work remain in their existing paths.

## Validation

- Current develop reproduced both stale `none` and stale `requested` failures through the real field registry, evaluator patch runner and loopback TCP catalog. In both cases the discarded decision suppressed the required fresh classifier.
- With the fix, the app-control suite passed **2,407 tests in 66 files**, including malformed/absent/unresolved/inactive replacement, valid replacement, single-use, independent-message, navigation and permission cases. Model responses in these tests are controlled inputs; this is not live-model acceptance.
- Repository verification passed **373/373 tasks and all final audits** on tree `435da135ac9e21e8d1f8b5a7c5844eeb03c5e439`, preserved by this head. Plugin build, typecheck and lint passed.
- Frozen install used the pinned Bun/Node versions. Initial checks stopped on missing generated keyword, prompts and UI output in the fresh worktree. Normal repository generation/build scripts supplied those artifacts; no source or lockfile change was needed. The initial failures remain recorded.

3c:T5bf,Mounted Notes interactions are rejected by the view capability broker because the production NOTES_SURFACE does not declare agent-surface. The canonical NOTES_VIEW uses that surface. The real Notes renderer loads its authenticated state successfully, but get-notes fails with ViewCapabilityDeniedError before any mutation can execute. Calendar already declares the required capability.

Reproduced in desktop Chromium and Pixel 7 mobile against candidate 6c1c25705fdb79e823717f9cb9ba835bd74b2485 while validating #30688. The app loads the emitted production Notes bundle and actual authenticated Notes API backed by isolated PGlite; surrounding shell identity/provider responses are deterministic fixtures. Both runs received a real empty Notes snapshot at revision 0 and then rejected the mounted read. No live-account or provider acceptance is claimed.

The existing DynamicViewLoader.notes-operation integration test passes a hardcoded agent-surface grant instead of the canonical Notes surface, masking the mismatch.

Fix: declare the intended grant on NOTES_SURFACE and use that production declaration in the existing mounted Notes test. Keep the capability broker unchanged and retain denial of guarded operations for legacy bundles lacking operation-context support. Acceptance includes the actual mounted read, create, exact replay, changed-payload rejection, stale-revision rejection, durable receipt identity, and unchanged rendered state after rejected edits.
3d:T469,The public `/pricing` page cannot scroll to its renewal disclosure and **Open billing** link on a 390×844 viewport when both subscription plans render. The application root constrains overflow, while `PricingPage` does not own a vertical scroll region. The lower content remains outside the viewport after real wheel input.

Reproduced with the actual built renderer at `db15e8b5f2b93d266e803ffd4bc20115f7314641` and controlled HTTP catalog responses for both v1 plans. Desktop is readable. The mobile browser regression fails `Open billing` viewport reachability after scrolling, with the lower content clipped in the captured frame. No checkout or provider operation was performed.

Expected: mobile visitors can scroll through both plans, read the renewal and purchased-credit disclosure, and reach the billing link. Preserve the existing desktop layout and pricing behavior.

Scope: give the existing pricing page its own scroll region and add a real browser reachability regression. Before/after screenshots, the original failing trace, and final source-bound validation will be linked from the combined billing repair PR.

Story Gate uses four shards with the same complete catalog. Duplicate personal-assistant/agent integrations remain in the general E2E runner. Develop Full removes 28 ineffective evidence-cache operations under its existing current-run-only policy. Exact-source evidence and successful completion remain prerequisites for the effect handoff. JS setup omits unneeded native/Python/protoc installation; existing native/manual consumers explicitly preserve prerequisites. Dependency caches restore only exact lock keys. Failure results are uploaded without inventing a successful manifest.

Type: CI performance and correctness improvements. No rendered UI, runtime prompts, product model behavior, credentials or production configuration changes.

## Known gaps / failures

Both are corrected with original formatting/boundary restored, and focused tests pass. App-core diagnostics were accidentally invoking the full chained package suite; the direct owning runner now executes the intended two tests. Device README wording was restored and checked.

Remaining old-checkpoint failures have concurrent owners: browser incomplete-read assertion (#31544); goals source-leaf resolution and OpenAI fixture (#31470/#31493 repair lane); scenario/When2Speak fixtures (#31493); LIFE integration isolation (#31032); Workerd expected tool catalog (embedding repair plus #31537 prerequisites). # Background

Recovered replies could describe an unsupported completed change; interrupted embedding downloads could leave completion unsettled; and trajectory copying reported success before the browser finished. The changes ground recovery against complete turn evidence, publish downloads only after complete file closure, and show the actual clipboard outcome while fencing older operations.

Also retain original dialogue/literal history receipts and stable cache prefixes, validate incomplete navigation declarations, preserve history reasoning controls through the provider wire, answer rejected time claims from CURRENT_TIME, reconcile legacy/single-account connector families, and provide WEB_SEARCH guidance after WEB_FETCH's actual guard deadline.

# Risks

Medium: recovery adds a recorded semantic reviewer call and optional full-context retry. Receipt validation and access revalidation remain required. The reviewer is fallible; these diagnostics are not a universal truth guarantee. The shared theme/contrast findings remain separate from the visible-focus repair.

42:T1383,Fixes #31437.

App inference settlement could reject a valid fractional markup refund: a $0.02 base reservation at 25% commits $0.0050 creator earnings, but settling $0.003 actual base cost requires a $0.0043 reversal to leave $0.0007. Rounding the raw difference independently gave incompatible ledger and projection amounts.

Version 2 receipts now record the original earning and initial/final collected creator amounts. Ledger movements use the difference between those endpoints while retaining the original six-decimal economic facts. Uncollected overage does not create creator entitlement; amounts below the ledger unit have explicit absent-ledger authority. The forward migration validates reservation/app/user/creator identity and protects the referenced original earning. Existing version 1 receipts and replay rules remain unchanged. Reservation-derived ledger keys use the locked canonical UUID, and app/user/organization UUID aliases are normalized without changing opaque idempotency keys.

The migration replaces the complete validator in one transaction to preserve its existing checks during the version transition. It is intentionally longer than typical additive migrations; splitting deployment would expose mixed validation contracts. Migration 0395 must run before deploying the new writer. The separate unpublished #31128 ledger migrations must be rebased and renumbered after this change lands.

The normal app-credit hold suite now applies the actual 0268 and 0395 migrations before exercising service calls, so receipt triggers and generated transaction keys are validated together. Reviewed validation repairs preserve real service exports, provision the schemas required by lifecycle reads, control expiry at the behavior being tested, and separate independent generated Docker shell scenarios. The JWT boundary fixture pins only its test clock while retaining real signatures and the production verifier.

43:T1256,## Summary

The evaluator rendered the planner's base context verbatim. The room roster (`ENTITIES`), the platform user record (`PLATFORM_USER_CONTEXT`), the widget syntax catalog (`uiWidgets`), the cross-room manifests (`recent-conversations`, `relevant-conversations`), `CHANNEL_TOPICS` and the first-run notice reached every in-loop evaluation call although `evaluatorTemplate` never references any of them.

Live evidence:
- Audit 2026-09-13: the evaluator user message was byte-identical to the planner's; `ENTITIES` + `PLATFORM_USER_CONTEXT` alone add ~7K chars per evaluator call.
- Live 2026-09-14: ~7.6K chars of `uiWidgets` / `recent-conversations` / `relevant-conversations` / `CHANNEL_TOPICS` / `firstRun` on every in-loop evaluation call.
- Gate proofs: the in-loop evaluator user message omits People in the Room, `PLATFORM_USER_CONTEXT` and the widget catalog but still carries `RECENT_MESSAGES`, `FACTS` and `CURRENT_TIME`; calendar create/move/delete evaluator FINISH still selects `effectReceiptIds`; RESTORE_CONTEXT reads refresh the evaluator context.

What changes:
- `provider-state.ts`: `EVALUATOR_STAGE_PROVIDER_EXCLUSIONS` names the providers the evaluator stage never reads.
- `pipeline.ts`: after the planner composition, `runV5MessageRuntimeStage1` composes a second `ContextObject` from the same already-run planner state through the existing `extraProviderExclusions` mechanism (the one ambient turns use), copies the planner composition's metadata onto it (`providerDiscoveryEnabled`, `historyReferenceEncoding`, `loadedContextProviders`, the completion-context selection), appends the same planner decision event and hands it to `runPlannerLoop` as `evaluatorContext`. The loaded context catalog reaches it too, so a reference the planner loaded never reads as still pending at evaluation time.
- `planner-types.ts`: `PlannerTrajectory.evaluatorBaseContext?` and `PlannerLoopParams.evaluatorContext?`.
- `planner-loop.ts`: the trajectory stores the evaluator composition (with the post-tool reply instruction appended when that seed is present); a planner-side `RESTORE_CONTEXT` read refreshes it through `restoreEvaluatorProviders` alongside `modelBaseContext`.
- `runtime/evaluator.ts`: `renderEvaluatorModelInput` renders `evaluatorBaseContext ?? modelBaseContext ?? context`, so callers that supply no evaluator composition render exactly as before; an evaluator `contextRequest` re-run refreshes both contexts; `restoreEvaluatorProviders` carries the restored planner provider bodies onto the evaluator composition while keeping its exclusions, without a second composition.

Policy note: this is not a cap or truncation. Whole named providers are removed via the existing `extraProviderExclusions` mechanism, and the removal is limited to the evaluator stage: the planner prompt is untouched, `evaluatorTemplate` never references the excluded providers, and `RESTORE_CONTEXT` reads are honored on both contexts (planner read and evaluator `contextRequest` re-run).

Derived from stable commits 51d30cd5d9f, 82c59fa5506 and the merge-only repairs in a3cbd5e9e26; carved onto `develop` (8e9fcf42d98) with only these hunks.

47:T713,Production backport of #31508 onto current `main` (`14d880c1b44`). The two affected runtime files were byte-identical on the production base and develop before this patch. This avoids promoting unrelated develop changes.

Provisioning-worker heartbeats created a new TCP Redis client on each cycle without releasing it. In production, one worker accumulated approximately 9,994 connections and exhausted Redis's 10,000-client limit, blocking lifecycle cleanup. Restarting the worker recovered the service but did not remove the leak.

Close Redis transports created by heartbeat publication, health reads, and capability checks in `finally`, including errors and timeouts. Caller-supplied clients retain caller ownership. Transport cleanup closes the socket directly instead of queuing `QUIT` behind stalled work. No funding, authorization, provisioning admission, or health-result policy changes.

Validation:
- New repeated/concurrent TCP connection regression fails against the prior implementation and passes with this change.
- 18 focused tests pass, covering connection cleanup, health behavior, socket recovery, errors, timeout and borrowed-client ownership.
- Cloud-shared package lint and typecheck pass (after building its login workspace dependency).
- Both the develop candidate and this exact production backport passed repository-wide `bun run verify` (exit 0). Hosted CI is pending. GitHub requires an eligible review before production merge; no deployment is claimed.
- Full `bun install` reached an unrelated native fused-inference CMake build failure after installing dependencies; this is not reported as a successful install.

Tracks the Redis blocker discovered during #31505 cleanup. Production recovery and live connection-count verification remain separate operational acceptance steps.

Cloud production pricing and authorization are unchanged; fixtures now use the current contracts.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Preserves full-parser equivalence when streamed stage directions trigger whitespace normalization across cached prose.
- Restores readable dark-theme muted text, selected trajectory counts, and Discord labels; uses the existing typed orange button variant for hosting publish and captures the actual empty trajectory state.
- Registers the public pricing entry and documents its public-route rationale; updates the HMR harness for the current developer workspace collaborator.
- Aligns Cloud billing expectations and dedicated-upgrade requests with current prices and explicit quote acceptance; regenerates SDK routes for subscription checkout.
- Runs the real login child from source in its restart/recovery test, prepares pinned native sources for provenance tests, and aligns CodeQL action versions.
- Uses the current canonical model in latency certification and gives the existing real PGlite scheduling case the same startup allowance as its neighboring tests.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes and CI fixture/tooling repairs. No test gate or authorization boundary is disabled.

# Documentation changes needed?

No public API or usage documentation changes required.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

The original failing develop run above and the linked evidence below. Production changes are confined to the streaming parser, public entry routing, UI colors, generated SDK routes, and latency model selection; the other changes repair tests or CI preparation.

- Normal install and `bun run verify`: pass, pinned runtimes.
- Storybook: 1,689 good; zero broken, accessibility violations, or gate failures. The 73 stories with HTTP 404 diagnostics are a subset of the baseline's 78; zero page errors.
- App audit: 226 checks pass; 220 captured views have good verdicts. OCR: 208 verified, 12 manually inspected fixture/unavailable states; zero regressions.

- Public entry/bootstrap: 38 pass. Public route allowlist: six pass.

4e:T29b7,# Defect

Retention pruning could delete an idempotency-key index entry that had already been rebound to a newer operation. While that newer operation was still inside the 24-hour idempotency window, a caller retry was accepted and executed again instead of being deduplicated. Provider switches or runtime restarts could therefore run twice after a network retry, UI double-submit, or CLI retry.

The repository now removes an idempotency mapping only when the mapping still points to the operation being pruned. A filesystem-backed regression locks that ownership rule.

# Relates to

No issue exists for this reproduced defect; this pull request is the complete defect report and fix.

# Sync with develop

- [x] Rebased onto `origin/develop` commit `3654ba0cff4928f1a72aa5c2c89c06898755255d` with zero conflicts.
- [x] `bun run verify` was run post-sync. Its unrelated missing-dependency blocker is recorded below.

# Risks

Low. The production change only guards deletion of an in-memory key-to-operation mapping. A mapping that still points to the pruned operation is deleted exactly as before; a mapping rebound to a different operation is preserved.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`byIdempotencyKey` is a key-to-operation-ID map. When a key is reused after its 24-hour dedupe window, `create()` correctly rebinds it to the new operation. The old record still retains the same key. `pruneTerminal()` previously deleted the key unconditionally while evicting that old record, corrupting the newer binding.

The fix compares the indexed operation ID with the ID being pruned before deleting the mapping.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

No documentation change is needed; this restores the documented idempotency behavior.

# Testing

## Before: reproduced duplicate execution

Command:

```text
bun run <provided-reproduction>/repro-idem2.ts
```

Observed output before the change:

```text
 Info       [runtime-ops] Accepted op ed8a0191-86e1-4b41-8753-4b52e042fb9c kind=provider-switch tier=hot
 Info       [runtime-ops] Operation ed8a0191-86e1-4b41-8753-4b52e042fb9c succeeded
first  : accepted ed8a0191-86e1-4b41-8753-4b52e042fb9c applies= 1
pruned : 1
old gone: true
 Info       [runtime-ops] Accepted op d49be3f8-4af3-44b2-912f-2036cd7bbaf3 kind=provider-switch tier=hot
 Info       [runtime-ops] Operation d49be3f8-4af3-44b2-912f-2036cd7bbaf3 succeeded
retry  : accepted d49be3f8-4af3-44b2-912f-2036cd7bbaf3 applies= 2
```

## After: retry deduplicates to the current operation

The supplied script hard-codes the shared checkout imports, so for isolated-worktree verification its two import paths were rewritten to the worktree through stdin; the reproduction logic was unchanged.

```text
 Info       [runtime-ops] Accepted op 66517079-d646-47ee-8f08-c2e34c5d70c6 kind=provider-switch tier=hot
 Info       [runtime-ops] Operation 66517079-d646-47ee-8f08-c2e34c5d70c6 succeeded
first  : accepted 66517079-d646-47ee-8f08-c2e34c5d70c6 applies= 1
pruned : 1
```
